And... well, that's pretty much when all of this started. I've been hearing his voice on and off for this whole past month since he died.\n\nDoctor: Besides hearing Garrett's voice, have you noticed anything else unusual, like seeing him, sensing his presence in other ways, or having thoughts that people are watching or against you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't seen him or... felt him around, if that's what you mean. It's really just his voice. And I don't feel like anyone is watching me or out to get me, either. It's just... hearing Garrett talk to me. I mean, obviously I was upset when Garrett passed, but... no, I haven't been feeling sad or down most of the day. It hasn't been like a constant low feeling or anything like that. It's really just been the stress of hearing him and everything falling apart because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the way you're thinking or speaking—like trouble organizing your thoughts, jumping between unrelated topics, or others telling you your behavior seemed unusual or disorganized during this past month?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think my thoughts have been disorganized, and nobody has mentioned anything about me jumping between topics or acting strange in that way. It's really just been the voice and the lack of sleep that have been the issues.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett's voice, what does he typically say to you, and does it ever feel threatening or instruct you to do things?\n\nPatient: He doesn't really tell me to do things, and... no, it's not threatening. It's not like he's giving me orders or trying to scare me. Usually, it's just... I'll hear him say my name. It happens when I'm fully awake. That's actually why I came in... I keep hearing Garrett’s voice as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. And even though part of me knows he’s dead, in the moment it feels completely real. It happens right in the middle of the day when I'm wide awake.\n\nDoctor: Aside from hearing Garrett's voice, have you ever heard any other voices or sounds that have nothing to do with him or your grief?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just Garrett. Like I said, that's why I'm here. I'll be out on a hike or in my painting room, and I hear his voice so clearly, like he's right there with me. I know he passed away, but when it happens, it just feels so real. I haven't heard anything else, just him.\n\nDoctor: Are you still hearing Garrett's voice now, in the past few days, or has it stopped since you came into the hospital?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's still happening. It really is just hearing Garrett's voice. That's the only unusual thing I've been experiencing.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett's voice, do you ever find yourself responding out loud, looking around for him, or acting as though he's actually there with you, or do you always know in that moment that it's not really happening?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying, in the actual moment... it just feels completely real. Even though the rational part of me knows he's passed away, the voice is so clear, like he's physically standing right behind me. So, yeah, I do look around for him. In that split second, I don't realize it's not happening. It just feels like he's actually there with me.\n\nDoctor: Counting from when Garrett passed away up until today, has it now been more than a full month that you've been hearing his voice, or are we still within that first month?\n\nPatient: It's been about a month. It happens when I'm fully awake. That's actually why I came in... I keep hearing Garrett’s voice as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. And even though part of me knows he’s dead, in the moment it feels completely real. It happens right in the middle of the day when I'm wide awake.\n\nDoctor: Aside from hearing Garrett's voice, have you ever heard any other voices or sounds that have nothing to do with him or your grief?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just Garrett. Like I said, that's why I'm here. I'll be out on a hike or in my painting room, and I hear his voice so clearly, like he's right there with me. I know he passed away, but when it happens, it just feels so real. I haven't heard anything else, just him.\n\nDoctor: Are you still hearing Garrett's voice now, in the past few days, or has it stopped since you came into the hospital?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's still happening. Like I mentioned a minute ago, that's the whole reason I came in... because I keep hearing his voice. It hasn't stopped since I've been here. It's still as clear as if he's standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. And even though part of me knows he's dead, whenever it happens, it just feels completely real. So, no, it hasn't gone away.\n\nDoctor: Aside from hearing Garrett's voice, have you had any other unusual beliefs or thoughts during this time—such as feeling like you have special powers, that people can read your mind, or that ordinary events have special hidden meanings for you?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't had any thoughts about having special powers or... or people reading my mind. And I haven't noticed any hidden meanings in ordinary things, either. It really is just hearing Garrett's voice. That's the only unusual thing I've been experiencing.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett's voice, do you ever find yourself responding out loud, looking around for him, or acting as though he's actually there with you, or do you always know in that moment that it's not really happening?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying, in the actual moment... it just feels completely real. Even though the rational part of me knows he's passed away, the voice is so clear, like he's physically standing right behind me. So, yeah, I do look around for him. I'll turn around on the trail or in my painting room expecting to see him right there. I might even start to say something back before I catch myself. In that split second, I don't realize it's not happening. It just feels like he's actually there with me.\n\nDoctor: Counting from when Garrett passed away up until today, has it now been more than a full month that you've been hearing his voice, or are we still within that first month?\n\nPatient: It's been about a month. Yeah, this whole crisis, or whatever you want to call it, has been unfolding for right around a month now, basically ever since he died.\n\nDoctor: Has there been any point in the past few days where the voice seemed less frequent or intense, or has it remained just as strong and constant since it first began a month ago?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't let up at all. It's just as strong. Like I said, I came in today because I just keep hearing his voice. It's still as clear as if he's standing right behind me, whether I'm out on the trail or just sitting in my painting room. It hasn't faded. Even though I know he's dead, it just... it still feels completely real every time it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me exactly how many days it's been since Garrett passed away and you first started hearing his voice—has it been less than 30 days, or more than a full month now?\n\nPatient: Like I said before, it's been about a month. Garrett died right around a month ago, and this whole crisis has been unfolding since then. So, I guess it's been about 30 days.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me the exact calendar date Garrett passed away, so we can count precisely how many days you've been hearing his voice?\n\nPatient: I don't have the exact calendar date memorized right now, honestly. I just know that this whole crisis has been unfolding for about a month since Garrett died. It's been roughly four weeks of this.\n\nDoctor: Can you help me pin down the timeline using a specific event—for example, do you remember what day the funeral was, or when exactly your wife called crisis services—so we can count precisely how many days it's been since Garrett passed?\n\nPatient: I don't have a specific date for the funeral or the call in my head right now. Like I've been saying, I just know that this whole crisis has been unfolding for about a month since Garrett died. It's been going on for roughly that long.\n\nDoctor: In between the times you hear Garrett's voice, would you say you feel completely like your normal self—thinking clearly, mood steady—or do you notice other changes in how you're functioning even when you're not hearing him?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say my mood is steady or normal at all. I actually only started hearing Garrett's voice during that really wired stretch... when I was barely sleeping, taking on all these impossible projects, and just feeling completely driven to keep going. I had never heard him like that before my mood shifted. It wasn't something that just happened during ordinary grief, it was tied directly to that whole period of being so wired.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period before this past month—at any other time in your life—where you felt unusually elevated, energetic, or needed very little sleep for several days, or conversely a distinct period of significant depression, before Garrett's passing?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really remember anything like that happening before, no. I mean, I've had normal ups and downs, like anyone, but not... not a distinct period where I didn't need sleep or felt completely wired like I did this past month. And no major depressions either, before Garrett died. This whole thing, the energy and the voice, it all just started recently."
